# Compositions test cleanup: partition 2

Pre-edit disposition record. Scope: all 41 files assigned in `/tmp/jasper-test-prune2/compositions2.txt`; 257 statically named test declarations plus the Sapphire wipe gallery loop (`spec.label`, every family entry). Dynamic `it.each`/scene/family declarations cover every expanded case. Counts are declaration counts, not coverage percentages.

Read AGENTS.md, package scripts, README setup/testing sections, architecture layer contracts, testing §2/3, renderer source and adjacent fixtures before disposition. Stable rendering seams are actual shader RGBA, Remotion PNG/Player pixels, and documented protocol data. Private pass names/counts and DOM scope trees are not contracts. Pixel tolerances absorb browser quantization; expected colors derive from synthetic input values, colour/alpha algebra, independent CPU references or committed goldens, never the shader being checked.

Unit failure inventory: metadata can miscompute rate/duration or zero duration; routing can choose an undecodable source; LUT loading can repeat fetch/hang on invalid input; worklet chunking/automation can differ from export; previews can omit frames or double audio; text-probe can lose text/font locations or hide unrelated content. Isolated tests remain only for these failure modes.

Validation plan: `pnpm exec vitest run <retained assigned paths> --maxWorkers=1`; `pnpm --filter @jasper/compositions typecheck`; changed real-render suites additionally use `REMOTION_E2E=1` and a working Chrome/ffmpeg. Full lint/typecheck/test/build is owned by integration. No claims of successful checks until appended below.

## Support and production seams planned for removal

Remove test-only exports `pictureLocalFrame` (production use remains within SequenceComposition) and `nestedScopeMessage` (production use remains within canvas-scope). Keep all helpers with production callers, including FxSlotCore (audio/worklet.ts), planLayerFx/fxCanvasLayout (fx/stage), flat-plan/composite-plan (compositor), cssExactAdjustment (SequenceComposition/NestLayer). Remove local fake-canvas/property/gallery fixtures alongside deleted tests; remove orphan GL harness smoke/dump/timing methods and unused imports returned only for deleted private assertions. No goldens are removed while their owning pixel tests survive.

## Completed cleanup and validation

Cleanup implemented across every assigned file: 83 DELETE, 24 REWRITE, 150 KEEP static declarations, plus DELETE for every dynamically named Sapphire wipe gallery case. Ten complete test files removed. Retained tests cover actual rendering pixels/goldens, preview audio range soundness, public metadata/media/LUT/text protocols, and foreign nested-canvas refusal. No retained failing test was deleted to make a run pass. No golden was re-blessed.

Removed support: mock canvas/planning fixtures; duplicate family/gallery fixture generation; manual PNG dumps/photo loaders/showcase options; family catalogue/default smoke/timing APIs; unused pass names/counts, canvas dimensions, temporal offset metadata, motion-blur tap metadata and benchmark shader injection; unused WebGL availability probes. Removed `pictureLocalFrame` and `nestedScopeMessage` exports, preserving their internal production use. Removed poison-hook imports in this partition; the composition-1 owner removes the shared hook and helper. Removed production CPU-derived OCIO expected values, allowing the project owner to remove uncalled CPU mirror functions.

Cross-review retained two initially disputed contracts: D-43 foreign canvas rejection has no stronger valid-scene owner, and randomized audio interval soundness complements the fixed nonempty-output examples. The OCIO rewrite now uses fixed IEC sRGB code values independently of the production CPU mirror.

Validation performed: targeted `pnpm exec biome check --write` passed for the 42 modified TypeScript/TSX files at that point; final targeted Biome passed all 43 modified TypeScript/TSX files, and `git diff --check` passed before commit. Focused Vitest, GL/Remotion suites, package typecheck and repository gates are explicitly pending root integration validation, per root's shared-host scheduling instruction (load exceeded 90; baseline typecheck still active). No baseline/product failures were observed in this partition because no tests were run here.

Integration documentation followups: `docs/testing.md` scene-distinctness guard and GL poison-hook descriptions are root-owned and must be removed; testkit owner is removing the now-unused `assertDistinctScenes`. No external documentation/source API contract is weakened by de-exporting the internal renderer helpers.

## REWRITE — published OCIO gray references move from the CPU mirror to shader pixels

Original declaration: `packages/project/test/fx-sapphire-adjust-composite.test.ts` — `S_OCIOTransform maths / matches published curve values`. The project partition removes the unused `ocioEncode` mirror; this declaration is **REWRITE across packages**, not loss of its independently published contract. Retained destination: `packages/compositions/test/fx-sapphire-adjust-composite-gl.test.ts` — `S_OCIOTransform encodes 18% gray at published reference levels`. This is one additional declaration migrated from the project partition; the original 257 composition-declaration dispositions above are unchanged.

Pre-edit failure inventory: wrong curve selection, damaged encoding constants, missing encode, or an unintended gamut tint can produce a visibly wrong neutral middle gray. The old CPU-only mirror could still pass when the GLSL version broke. Existing IEC sRGB cases do not exercise the LogC3, S-Log3, V-Log, ACEScct or Rec.709 encoders; default scene fingerprints do not establish these published calibration points. Non-test caller: `jasper.s.ocio-transform` runs through `FxPicture` / the shader host in Program preview and export. Risk: 8-bit input quantization and GPU readback; tolerance is three code values, no reference changes/blessing. Validation command: `pnpm exec vitest run packages/compositions/test/fx-sapphire-adjust-composite-gl.test.ts --maxWorkers=1`; GL execution remains pending root's scheduled integrated run.

Primary sources checked before the rewrite:

| Curve | Independent 18% gray source | Literal 8-bit expected output |
| --- | --- | --- |
| LogC3 EI800 | [ARRI ALEXA Log C Curve — Usage in VFX, p. 3](https://www.arri.com/resource/blob/31918/66f56e6abb6e5b6553929edf9aa7483e/2017-03-alexa-logc-curve-in-vfx-data.pdf): normalized 0.391, also given as 400/1023 | 100 |
| S-Log3 | [Sony technical summary, Appendix](https://download.pro.sony/FNGP/protein/1237494271390/1237494271406.pdf): gray at 420/1023 | 105 |
| V-Log | [Panasonic V-Log/V-Gamut reference manual, Fig. 2.2](https://pro-av.panasonic.net/cn/cinema_camera_varicam_eva/support/pdf/VARICAM_V-Log_V-Gamut.pdf): gray at 433/1023 | 108 |
| ACEScct | [Academy ACEScct encoding specification, Equation 1](https://docs.acescentral.com/encodings/acescct/): evaluation at 0.18 gives approximately 0.4135884 | 105 |
| Rec.709 | [ITU-R BT.709-6, table 1 item 1.2](https://www.itu.int/dms_pubrec/itu-r/rec/bt/R-REC-BT.709-6-201506-I%21%21PDF-E.pdf): evaluation at 0.18 gives approximately 0.409008 | 104 |

The last two values are calculations from the published equations, not claimed tabulated reference points. The fixture uses equal linear RGB bytes of 46 (`46/255 ≈ 0.180392`), the closest 8-bit value to 18% gray. Expected bytes are fixed literals obtained by rounding the independently published normalized levels multiplied by 255; the ±3 tolerance covers the input approximation and output quantization. No encode/decode formula is implemented in the test or expected-value harness.

Six-item bar: (1) ARRI, Sony, Panasonic, Academy and ITU explicitly define these transfer curves and middle-gray levels; (2) a wrong displayed/exported gray or neutral tint changes the observed RGB bytes; (3) literal expected values come from the sources above and can disagree with both CPU and GLSL code; (4) the test renders the registered public effect's From/To parameters through the actual shader host and reads output RGBA; (5) shader decomposition, uniform names and helper refactors may change without affecting the test; (6) actual GPU output is the lowest stable seam for these GLSL curves, and the removed unused CPU mirror plus existing sRGB/fingerprint tests do not own these exact points.

Follow-up static validation: targeted Biome passed both changed TypeScript files; `git diff --check` passed. GL and typecheck were not run in this worktree because root reserved runtime validation for the integrated suite.

## Final oracle-independence rescan (pre-edit record)

Rescan base: integrated `cf890a29`. Reviewed retained partition expectations and their GL/browser harnesses, with special attention to imported production references. The following dispositions supersede the earlier oracle descriptions where noted.

- **KEEP** `audio-fx-preview.test.ts`, both preview/export tests: AGENTS §4 explicitly requires equal preview/export audio. `FxSlotCore` is called by the AudioWorklet; `EffectSlot` is called by the native mixer. Running the same processor with 128-sample worklet chunks and irregular export chunks is the contract, not an independent oracle for DSP frequency response. A state reset, block-boundary bug or mismatched automation cadence can make the actual output streams differ. Expected equality is independently specified, survives processor refactors, and the worklet/mixer seam is the lowest owner of this relationship. Audio-fx signal tests independently own processor correctness.
- **KEEP** `color.test.tsx / matches the independent project LUT reference and leaves alpha untouched`: S06 §3.2 explicitly names `applyLut` as the tetrahedral interpolation reference. Caller census: `applyLutRgba8` is a separate optimized implementation in `compositions/src/color/cpu.ts`, used by the CPU render stage; it does not call `applyLut`. Sharing the input LUT is intentional. A stride, tetrahedron, rounding or alpha defect in the optimized byte-array implementation disagrees with the scalar reference. This is the lowest numeric seam for S06:CG-043 and survives backend refactors.
- **KEEP** `blend-gl.test.ts / GL compositor = blendPixel: %s` and CSS pixel comparisons: AGENTS §4 and `docs/wp-notes/compositor.md` require the GL blend scope to equal the native kernel. Census: JS `blendPixel`/`dissolveNoise`/`storePremul8` serve native/Classic3D, while GL uses separately implemented `fx_layerBlend`/`fx_blendNoise` GLSL. Shader output cannot call the expected JS calculation. The test owns cross-backend alpha, opacity, quantization and mode parity, not the source formula's independent correctness. A bug in one backend disagrees; project blend numeric tests independently pin formulas. Actual shader/Chrome output is the stable lowest cross-backend seam.
- **KEEP** `golden-scenes.e2e.test.ts`, all scenes: each output still matches an independently stored reviewed PNG. Computed references are additional checks for compositing order/backend parity. In particular, `combo-hdr-display-scope` applies the production display LUT to the export signal as an expected *wiring* result; it is not an independent proof of that LUT's color science. A common LUT defect is caught by the committed golden and exact public HDR numeric tests. No golden is re-blessed and no production code changes.
- **REWRITE** `compositor-player.e2e.test.ts / a nest with effects whose nested sequence nests scopes draws as a canvas source`: replace the single production `blendPixel` expectation with literal `[26, 0, 26]`. For the declared opaque `[0.8,0.4,0.2]` and `[0.3,0.6,0.9]` solids, linear burn yields `[0.1,0,0.1]`; the inner and outer Invert cancel. Failure: nesting/capture/order renders the wrong color. Independent source: linear-burn mode definition and compositor D-43; no other live Player test owns this nested-canvas seek. The literal oracle can disagree with both CPU and GL math, observes actual screenshot pixels, survives DOM/internal renames, and remains at the live scheduling seam. Risk: byte quantization, existing tolerance four; validation is the same opt-in Player suite.
- **REWRITE** `lumetri-spatial-gl.test.ts / HSL Secondary key + correction equals the CPU reference on every pixel`: `lumetriSpatialSettings` is shared between the test oracle and registry shader uniforms, so wrong normalization/section gating can pass both. Replace it with hand-painted red/blue/gray inputs and literal selected/unselected/inverted results, plus explicit hue/lightness softness and three-way highlight contracts transferred from project tests. Source: `docs/wp-notes/color-grade.md` §2 HSL Secondary and the existing independent project cases. Failures: incorrect selector, ignored invert/saturation, wrong hue/lightness band, or highlight correction. No stronger GPU owner exists for these exact selector controls. Real RGBA is the lowest seam; literals independent of production settings survive refactors. Risk: 8-bit quantization, tolerance at most three for fractional keys.
- **REWRITE** `lumetri-spatial-gl.test.ts / Show Mask (Color/Gray, Color/Black, White/Black) equals the reference`: retain named mask semantics on independently painted red/blue halves, asserting exact selected color/white and excluded black/neutral gray rather than sharing production settings/reference code. These modes are specified in the same work note. A wrong shader mask style fails visibly; the selector/mask output seam is stable across shader reorganization. No stronger output test owns all three modes. The Color/Gray test asserts a neutral visible gray for the excluded region without copying the implementation's particular gray remapping formula.
- **REWRITE** `lumetri-spatial-gl.test.ts / the vignette equals the reference for every roundness and feather`: remove CPU-equality loops; keep the independent center/corner behavior and transfer positive/negative extreme and roundness behavior from the removed CPU mirror owner. Source: work note §2 Vignette. Failure: wrong corner brightening/darkening, changed center or ignored shape. The actual GPU pixels are the lowest stable contract owner; signs/unchanged center/relative roundness follow the documented controls rather than CPU settings. No replacement formula is introduced.

Lumetri caller census: the registry uses `lumetriSpatialSettings`; the `lumetriSpatialPixel` expected-output function is used only by this test harness and project `lumetri-grade.test.ts`. Project owner coordinates removal of dead CPU-only output helpers and transfer of its independently specified cases. This partition removes the test harness's `reference` flag, CPU comparison loop, derived max-difference output, and unused hue chart after the rewrite. Focused validation command: `pnpm exec vitest run packages/compositions/test/lumetri-spatial-gl.test.ts --maxWorkers=1`; runtime remains queued at integration.

Additional assigned rescan: `fx-sapphire-distort-warp-gl.test.ts`. Its transparency and magnifier tests observe independent fixed RGBA/region conditions, not production constants. The identity test and two lens-map tests currently use a WarpTransform render as their source oracle; replace that expected source with the hand-painted input canvas before any shader. This is **REWRITE** for `identity settings give the source back`, `S_WarpDrops ripples around the bright spots of the Centers map only`, and `S_Distort follows the Lens: a flat lens changes nothing, the dots lens warps`. The public zero-amplitude/identity/constant-lens contracts require unchanged fixture pixels; direct fixture readback can disagree with a common shader fault. It survives shader-plan changes and is the lowest observable pixel seam; remaining fingerprint tests exercise different controls. No production seam is added. Risk: source/output quantization; existing tolerances retained. Root observed three 120-second watchdog failures under load 155/16 cores; these remain validation failures requiring a lower-load rerun, and are not a reason for deleting or relaxing tests.

**REWRITE across packages** — `packages/project/test/lumetri-grade.test.ts` spatial-stage tests (hue/lightness softness, selected correction/Invert/mask, 3-way highlights, vignette) and HSL eyedropper tests (`Set Color keys the picked colour, not others; greys key on lightness only`, `Add Color extends the key to the new colour and keeps the old one`, `Remove Color drops the picked colour and keeps the rest of the key`). Destination: the rewritten Lumetri GL tests plus `HSL eyedroppers select the picked colors through the rendered mask`. `keyFromColor`, `addColorToKey`, `removeColorFromKey` remain production APIs used by the Lumetri eyedropper UI. Only their generated **input parameters** are passed to the shader; expected selected/rejected mask bytes are independent literal white/black. Sources: work note §2 HSL Secondary eyedropper Set/Add/Remove semantics. Wrong hue/range expansion or contraction causes a picked color to be rejected or a removed color to remain selected; that is visible in the actual mask. The imported key builders are part of the action under test, never the expectation. Thus (1) documented selection semantics, (2) actual wrong mask pixels, (3) literal independent oracle, (4) UI key builder to registered shader output, (5) survives all helper/pass renames, and (6) lowest seam establishing the produced range settings select the intended pixels. The old CPU helper owner is removed by project4; no additional duplicate tests remain there. Risk is byte quantization around an intentionally chosen half-mask, limited to tolerance three; nonboundary picked colors are asserted exactly.


The final caller census also finds `fx-sapphire-distort-warp/entry.ts` photo loading (`images`, `loadImage`, `img:` painting), PNG serialization, and returned private `canvas`/`box` metadata unconsumed after cleanup. Its only caller is `fx-sapphire-distort-warp-gl.test.ts`, which reads `probes` and `checksum`; there are no production callers. **DELETE support** before commit: remove those unused paths and preserve the independently painted fixture plus actual shader readback. Risk is limited to obsolete manual test diagnostics; focused validation is the retained distort GL suite.

Exact transfer map (old path `packages/project/test/lumetri-grade.test.ts`, new path `packages/compositions/test/lumetri-spatial-gl.test.ts`):

| --- | --- |

| `the correction applies inside the key only; Show Mask and Invert` | `HSL Secondary corrects only the selected colors and supports Invert` and `Show Mask preserves selected color or white and marks excluded pixels gray or black`: fixed desaturated RGB, unchanged excluded RGB, inverse selection and the three displayed mask styles. |

| `Set Color keys the picked colour, not others; greys key on lightness only` | `HSL eyedroppers select the picked colors through the rendered mask`: orange selected, blue excluded; near-gray selected by a gray pick and bright gray excluded. |
| `Add Color extends the key to the new colour and keeps the old one` | Same eyedropper destination: after Add, both orange and red are white and blue remains black. |
| `Remove Color drops the picked colour and keeps the rest of the key` | Same eyedropper destination: after Remove, red is black and orange remains white. The old assertion that a parameter object changes is discarded; visible selection owns the behavior. |

## Focused runtime investigation: S_MatteOps

The integrated full run reported a failure in `packages/compositions/test/fx-sapphire-adjust-composite-gl.test.ts / S_MatteOps: shrink/grow with every filter, invert, outputs, post blur, deterministic noise` after approximately 86 seconds. The case and its expected values were unchanged by cleanup. Its suite declares a **60-second** test timeout; only `beforeAll` has a 120-second timeout. The production owner remains `packages/project/src/registry/fx/sapphire-adjust-composite.ts` with its matte shaping functions in `sapphire-adjust-composite/matte.ts`.

A fresh isolated worktree at integrated `4e96e3c9` installed dependencies successfully with `pnpm install --frozen-lockfile --offline`, then ran only:

```sh
pnpm exec vitest run packages/compositions/test/fx-sapphire-adjust-composite-gl.test.ts --maxWorkers=1 -t 'S_MatteOps:'
```

Result: one selected test failed, eight cases were intentionally excluded by the name filter. The selected case failed after 63,391 ms with `Error: Test timed out in 60000ms` at test line 118. The `afterAll` browser-close hook also failed with `Error: Hook timed out in 10000ms` at line 31. There was no pixel, alpha, noise-determinism or seed/frame assertion failure. Total run duration was 211.41 seconds, including 37.04 seconds importing and 160.96 seconds running the file. The 16-core shared host's observed one-minute load was 137–165 during setup/reproduction.

This focused result demonstrates timeout failures under heavy host load, not a numerical product defect. It does not establish a passing regression or replace the original full-run stack, which was not yet available during this investigation. No test, expected value, timeout, production owner or disposition was changed. The regression remains KEEP and requires a lower-load rerun. Vitest exited with code 1; no Chrome process rooted in the isolated worktree remained afterward. Only this evidence note changed, and `git diff --check` passed.
